| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.


# The document under review (round 1 snapshot)

# brt-vbi — delivery of arbitrary-width runtime helper bodies

Council proposal, not a specification or runtime implementation. Origin:
`brt-vbi` (blocked decision), blocking `brt-zct` in bennett-runtimes.
References: [dialect 0.2.12](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md)
§§12.1, 12.7–12.8, 13.1, 19.1, and the corresponding
[0.3 draft](../../../../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md).

## Exact defect and North Star scope

§12.7 names width-specific ordinary runtime bodies for wide multiply,
divide, remainder and shifts. §12.8 similarly names integer intrinsic
helpers, including slot-based wide forms. §19.1 requires matching-major
runtime bitcode before materialisation. §13.1 publishes an offline
instance-request protocol for arith, but none of these clauses publishes
a runtime-integer request/response contract or a collection procedure for
helpers introduced during normalisation. A consumer cannot depend on
the runtime producer's private generator interface.

This is a **dialect delivery/interoperability gap**, alongside an actual
missing-implementation obligation. It is not a new ISA arithmetic
instruction or launch ABI gap. Naming an ordinary helper already makes
its body required; a finite default library failing to contain i257
does not make that LLVM width unsupported. The gap matters to North Star
§§3.2–3.3, 5.1's whole materialised LLVM and reproducible comparison.
It does not justify narrowing widths, trapping valid arithmetic, omitting
libc/Julia/SQLite closure, or declaring the broader brt-zct work complete.

## Alternatives and concrete witness

Read-only source witness:
`../bennett-runtimes/.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/tmp/runtime-width-request.ll`.
It loads two i257 values, multiplies, calls `llvm.ctpop.i257`, stores i257.
Both LLVM 18 and 23 `opt -passes=verify -disable-output` accept it.
Both live `dist/llvm<M>/bennett-runtimes.bc` lack definitions for
`__bennett_rt_i257_mul` and `__bennett_rt_i257_ctpop`, confirmed with
matching-major `llvm-nm --defined-only`. These are current observations,
not an inference from the producer's progress report.

For a concrete numerical vector, a=2^256+3, b=2. Modulo 2^257 the product
is 6 and its population count is 2. A five-limb input slot has widths
[64,64,64,64,1]; ctpop writes [2,0,0,0,0]. Dropping the top input limb
can be caught separately by ctpop(a)=3. This requires actual ordinary
bodies, not a declaration that merely passes LLVM verification.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Publish an offline request/manifest protocol in the dialect | Producer-independent, analogous to arith; recommended. |
| Prebuild every admitted width and operation | A possible complete distribution, but costly; a common-width subset is only a cache. |
| Publish a separate runtime ABI note | Viable only if versioned and normatively incorporated by the dialect; a producer README alone cannot bind competitors. |
| Add ISA primitives or a private driver-generator API | Unnecessary machine extension or unpublished coupling. |

## Recommended exact dialect/ABI text

Insert the following titled subsection at the end of §12.8 before the
examples, and cross-reference it from §19.1. Mirror it in both tracks.
The executable name below is a proposed public interface, not a claim
that today's runtimes supplies it.

```text
Runtime integer instance delivery v1.

The runtimes distribution MUST supply an offline executable interface:
  bennett-runtimes-instantiate --request REQUEST --bitcode OUTPUT_BC
                             --manifest OUTPUT_JSON
The driver may configure the executable path. Exit zero means both
outputs are complete; nonzero means no output is usable. Generation
must not require network access, a host runtime service or private
consumer knowledge. The distribution MUST identify its version.

REQUEST is UTF-8 JSON with exactly schema, llvm_major, target_triple,
data_layout and entries. schema is "bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1";
llvm_major is the link's integer LLVM major; target_triple and
data_layout are the link's effective LLVM strings. Each entries element
has exactly op (string) and width (integer, not boolean).

The op set is mul, udiv, sdiv, urem, srem, shl, lshr, ashr from §12.7;
and bswap, bitreverse, ctpop, ctlz, cttz, fshl, fshr, sshl_sat,
ushl_sat, smul_fix, umul_fix, smul_fix_sat, umul_fix_sat, sdiv_fix,
udiv_fix, sdiv_fix_sat, udiv_fix_sat, pdep, pext, clmul,
smul_with_overflow and umul_with_overflow from §12.8. Dots in an
intrinsic name become underscores. width is the original integer
operand width W, not a limb width or result aggregate size. The
operation and width must be admitted by the link's LLVM major and the
corresponding published row; §12.7 requests require W>128, and the
overflow-helper requests require W>64. Unknown fields/operations,
nonintegral/out-of-range widths and target/major/layout mismatches are
errors. Every valid request remains an implementation obligation.

Duplicates denote one instance. Canonical request bytes are UTF-8 JCS,
without a trailing LF, with unique entries sorted by op's unsigned
UTF-8 bytes then ascending numeric width. request_sha256 is the
lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 of those bytes. A supplied width list
or prebuilt cache MUST NOT become a capability limit.

OUTPUT_BC is ordinary matching-major bitcode whose triple and
DataLayout equal the request. For each requested (op,W), provide the
exact public definition, type, scalar flags, result convention and
fault behavior of §§12.7–12.8. In particular, wide mul is
void @__bennett_rt_i<W>_mul(ptr dst,ptr a,ptr b), and wide ctpop is
void @__bennett_rt_i<W>_ctpop(ptr dst,ptr a). The existing shadow-stack
slot layout, partial top limb and push/store/call/load/pop order are
unchanged. All reachable implementation bodies must be supplied or
resolved through the published matching-major base runtime/arith
distributions and their published instance protocols. No unresolved
instance placeholder, trap stub or hidden host implementation counts
as delivery.

OUTPUT_JSON is JCS with exactly schema, request_sha256,
bitcode_sha256 and exports. schema is "bennett-runtimes-instances-1";
bitcode_sha256 hashes the exact OUTPUT_BC bytes. exports lists every
externally visible definition in that module, sorted by unsigned UTF-8
symbol bytes, each with exactly symbol and function_type; function_type
is LLVM's printed function type for the requested major, without the
function name or attributes. Every requested public symbol must occur
exactly once and have its published type. Generation with identical
canonical request and producer version must reproduce both output
byte strings regardless of output paths. The driver verifies hashes,
triple, layout, definitions and types before using the output.

Collection is a link-preparation fixed point. Starting from the selected
device entries and their conservatively reachable bitcode closure,
include calls already naming these helpers and all helpers that the
published normalisation rules will introduce, including vector
scalarisation, N27 and the N22 applications after N27/N32. Collect
arith instances by §13.1 as well. Resolve runtime/arith requests and
their dependencies until no new (provider,instance) is required and
every required body is linked. Include all possible device call
targets; do not select just one runtime input path. Declarations alone
do not satisfy the fixed point. A provider cycle must resolve to actual
linked definitions; repeated unresolved requests are a loud failure.

This discovery is a non-emitting analysis using the published rules;
it does not rerun or change §12.1's normative rule order. Link the
complete collected bodies before final materialisation, identically
in the lifted and native comparison inputs. If later optimisation or
materialisation reveals a new required helper, restart preparation
with the enlarged request and rebuild both outputs; never patch only
one build or silently leave it unresolved. Failure to generate a
valid instance is an implementation blocker, not an unsupported-width
verdict, narrower substitute or permitted fault of the device program.
```

Add to §19.1 after the bitcode-source paragraph:

```text
Before final materialisation the driver MUST complete the runtime
integer instance-delivery fixed point of §12.8 and the arith requests
of §13.1, using the same resolved closure in both builds. Whole-program
device mode uses its entire device closure; selective-module mode
collects the union required by its marked device roots. Neither mode
requires host-only code to become a device region.
```

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Request and output hashes are different identities; neither is an ISA
`object_hash` or the arith/ref catalog hash. Provider-version changes
invalidate the generation cache even when the request is identical.
Generated bitcode changes materialised closures, costs, stack use and
the resulting object/schedule/certificate bindings. Drivers, plugins,+runtimes, numerical providers and conformance owners must coordinate
publication and repin. No ISA `ref@0.9` recipe change is necessary.

## Conformance vectors and actual verification

The bounded command in [INDEX](INDEX.md) ran both major-specific LLVM
verifiers and symbol audits: exit 0, both missing-definition assertions
passed. This establishes the delivery witness, not helper implementation
or whole-program execution. No sibling gate was run.

After publication require: i129/i192/i257 partial-top-limb values;
mul and ctpop together; large admitted widths with a resource-bounded
generation test; narrow intrinsic helpers; scalar flags and overflow
aggregate types; all helper families with published fault behavior.
Check reordered/duplicate requests canonicalise identically, unknown
op/invalid width/major/layout reject loudly, manifests cannot omit or
mis-type a requested definition, and changed request/bitcode hashes fail.
Test a helper introduced only by scalarisation, one after N32/repeated
N22, a transitive dependency and a discovery restart. Verify byte
reproduction in relocated directories and link/execute both materialised
builds. Merely passing opt verification is insufficient.

## K entry and publication route

**No ISA 0.9/0.10 K entry:** this is a dialect/tool-distribution contract.
Proposed dialect decision row: `RUNTIME-INSTANCES-1 | §§12.8,19.1 |
offline request/manifest and complete device-closure fixed point |
brt-vbi | additive versioned delivery protocol`. Recommend the next
published 0.2 patch and the corresponding 0.3 draft text. A separate
ABI note is acceptable only with a normative versioned dialect reference.
Leave brt-vbi/brt-zct open for ratification and implementation; publication
of a proposal does not unblock a consumer's unpublished interface.

## Mixed fat object

[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md) binds this
proposal (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). Collect helpers for marked modules and
their linked device closures. Multiple roots may share generated bodies;
each device root retains its own certificate/context association. The
ordinary native host binary, host entry/argv/envp and host-only libc stay
outside that inventory. Native comparison binds the matching materialised
device closure. Request manifests are build inputs, not a new launch,
budget, handle or effect-tape protocol. Container/root association and
host-memory mapping still need the exact mixed-launch additions named in
the shared scope note; this delivery decision keeps them reachable.

codex
I’ll use the repository’s beads guidance and read the cited clauses, witness, and publication rules before grading the diff.

 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.
---

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# Contract derivations 3 — council decision index

Five proposals, not published spec changes or consumer acceptance. Read
COMMON, repository CLAUDE/AGENTS, the Beads skill/prime, two first-pass
notes and two pass2 notes before derivation. All five full issue records
were read with bd. Direct sibling reads, including --readonly, failed
at the read-only embedded Dolt LOCK. Copies of the actual embedded
databases under `.tmp/sol-derive3/` then permitted `bd --readonly show`
for 4yg.1 and brt-vbi. No passive JSONL was imported or substituted for
the database. Source issues remain open for council/implementation work.

| Issue | Determination | Recommended publication |
|---|---|---|
| [bennett-arith-4yg.1](bennett-arith-4yg.1.md) | Valid FMA and binary128-conversion midpoints have no even candidate under the printed PPC rule; flags separately violate an existing rule | Dialect §13.1 parity repair in next 0.2 patch and 0.3 draft; no ISA K |
| [brt-vbi](brt-vbi.md) | Published all-width helper requirements lack a public delivery/collection protocol; actual i257 bodies also missing | Dialect §§12.8/19.1 offline interface and fixed point; no new ISA opcode/K |
| [b-isa-spec-pe1](b-isa-spec-pe1.md) | K13 example invents a borrowed lifetime that K70 rejects | 0.9 K13 example erratum: empty borrowed arrays, 24-bit D |
| [b-isa-spec-2qc](b-isa-spec-2qc.md) | Executor preflight and proof owner exist; certificate checker coverage duty is ambiguous | Explicit owner/site/context rule, coordinated with 0jq; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |
| [b-isa-spec-sqr](b-isa-spec-sqr.md) | Bundle entries in an mcx list have no explicit operand rule; current implementations disagree | Ordered bundle expansion, full wire arity/distinctness; council-authorized 0.9 K repair or 0.10 |

None is an excuse to reduce a format/width domain, omit an inverse
obligation, or declare a North Star capability inert. K13 is an example
defect rather than a new machine capability gap. The remaining findings
concern deterministic contract interpretation or delivery. All existing
SQLite, Julia, libc, reversible execution and demo-ladder obligations
remain. Existing beads and cross-repo requests already track the decisions;
no duplicate task or sibling tracker mutation is needed for this pass.

## Publication and combined decisions

[spec/README.md](../../../spec/README.md) requires a full version change
for semantic changes and council review. “0.9 K repair” in these notes
means a proposed use of the existing council repair process, **not
authority to silently amend a published contract**. Without explicit
in-version authorization, the 2qc/sqr semantic additions go to 0.10.
New version prefixes change affected object/interface/closure envelopes,
example identities and consumer pins. A same-version repair with unchanged
source bytes preserves their source identities but still invalidates
incompatible execution/schedule/proof assumptions. `ref@0.9` changes only
if its own catalog bytes/version change; none of these proposals requires
such a rewrite. Assign numeric K entries only at acceptance.

PPC parity and runtime delivery belong to the dialect because they define
LLVM numerical/helper lowering and build inputs. ISA 0.9/0.10 need no
new K for either. Publish the exact proposed dialect text in the next
available maintained patch and mirror it in 0.3; the proposal is not a
private runtime/driver agreement. The new helper protocol is itself
versioned with schema 1, independent of ISA subformat numbers.

For 2qc use the existing concrete invocation-context vocabulary, owner
closure selection and owner-local claim indices. Coordinate with
[pass2 0jq](../2026-11-02-pass2/b-isa-spec-0jq.md); do not create a second
inverse proof owner or replace tuple applicability by root budget equality.
For sqr retain the written source identity and resolve complete wire
operands before K78 primitive matching and scheduling. A parenthesized
indexed list is not currently a legal substitute under §16.2.

## Mixed fat object

Every note cites and applies
[fat-object-scope.md](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md), binding under
bennett-4ht and advisor 08:45Z (North Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). A native host
binary remains native; only marked device modules and their required
linked closures become ISA objects. Helper generation, comparison,
certificate inventory and invocation coverage bind those device roots,
with a separate root-relative context [] per root. They do not inventory
native main, host-only argv/envp setup or host-only libc. The shared note
names the exact remaining container/root/certificate association, launch,
budget/handle, tape/run and host-memory mapping changes. This pass neither
implements those mechanisms nor locks them out.

## Reproducible finite checks

Run the following shell block after the environment prefix. It extracts
the Python below from this index and verifies the actual notes plus
concrete witnesses; it creates only a scratch ccx object. The outer memory
bound is inherited by all child commands.

```sh
date -u
ulimit -v 1048576
ulimit -c 0
timeout 120 python3 -I -B -u - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-derive3/INDEX.md')

exec(compile(code, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

```python
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from itertools import product
import hashlib, json, os, re, subprocess, sys

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
WS = ROOT.parent
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-derive3'
JOB = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-derive3'
REF = JOB / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
REPLAY = WS / 'bennett-conformance/checker-replay'
WITNESS = REPLAY / 'witnesses/mcx-bundle-control'
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(JOB / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(JOB / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))
ids = ['bennett-arith-4yg.1', 'brt-vbi', 'b-isa-spec-pe1',
       'b-isa-spec-2qc', 'b-isa-spec-sqr']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {i+'.md' for i in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    s = (OUT / (ident+'.md')).read_text()
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
    for heading in ('defect', 'north star', 'alternatives', 'recommended exact',
                    'hashes', 'conformance', 'k entry', 'mixed fat object'):
        assert heading in s.lower(), (ident, heading)
    assert 'fat-object-scope.md' in s
    assert index.count('| ['+ident+']') == 1
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
print('PASS: five proposals and index; sections, links, fences, fat-object citations')

def pow2(n):
    return F(2**n) if n >= 0 else F(1, 2**(-n))
def decode(bits, fraction_bits, exponent_bits, bias):
    sign = -1 if bits >> (fraction_bits+exponent_bits) else 1
    exp = (bits >> fraction_bits) & ((1 << exponent_bits)-1)
    frac = bits & ((1 << fraction_bits)-1)
    assert exp != (1 << exponent_bits)-1
    sig = frac if exp == 0 else (1 << fraction_bits) | frac
    return sign * sig * pow2((1-bias if exp == 0 else exp-bias)-fraction_bits)
def f64(bits):
    return decode(bits, 52, 11, 1023)
u = pow2(-1074)
h = f64(0x3ff0000000000001)
assert h == 1+pow2(-52)
exact = (h+u)*F(1,2) + f64(0x3fe0000000000001)
assert exact == h+u/2
H = f64(0x0030000000000001)
V = decode(0x3c030000000000001200000000000000, 112, 15, 16383)
assert V == H+u/2 and H > pow2(-1022)
for hi, v in ((h, exact), (H, V)):
    assert (hi/u).denominator == 1
    assert v-hi == hi+u-v == u/2
    assert hi+u-hi == u
# The two high raw significands and the nonzero low significand are odd.
assert (0x3ff0000000000001 & 1) == (0x0030000000000001 & 1) == 1
assert (0 & 1, 1 & 1) == (0, 1)  # proposed common low comparison selects zero
# Both-low-zero subnormal neighbours still use high parity.
assert f64(2)-f64(1) == u and (1 & 1, 2 & 1) == (1, 0)
print('PASS: independent exact PPC FMA/conversion midpoints and parity distinction')

spec = (ROOT / 'spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md').read_text()
block = spec.split('**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**', 1)[1].split('```json\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
assert block.count('"borrowed":["1"]') == 6
fixed = block.replace('"borrowed":["1"]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('"borrowed":[1]', '"borrowed":[]')
fixed = fixed.replace('1/33554432', '1/16777216')
k13 = json.loads(fixed)
tuples = [k13['row']['before'], k13['row']['after'],
          k13['wrong_row']['before'], k13['wrong_row']['after'],
          k13['changed_arg_after'], k13['short_budget_after']]
assert all(t['borrowed'] == [] and t['regions'] == [['2','1']] for t in tuples)
assert k13['widths']['borrowed'] == []
bits = sum(k13['widths']['bundles']) + sum(sum(r) for r in k13['widths']['regions'])
assert bits == 24 and str(F(1, 1 << bits)) == k13['covered_fraction']
assert 2 ^ ((1+2) % 4) == 1 and 2 ^ ((1+1) % 4) == 0
assert (2 ^ 0, 3 ^ 1) == (2, 2)
assert k13['sample_count'] == 4 and k13['distinct_samples'] == 1
print('PASS: all six K13 tuple repairs; 24-bit domain and exact singleton coverage')

# A finite coverage counterexample, not an implementation of the checker.
minimum = (3,0,0,0)
def covers(tk, arg, domain=range(4), token_arg=None):
    return (all(a <= b for a,b in zip(minimum,tk)) and arg in domain
            and (token_arg is None or token_arg == (3,0,0,0)))
assert covers((3,0,0,0),1) and covers((9,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((2,0,0,0),1)
assert not covers((3,0,0,0),3,range(3))
assert not covers((9,0,0,0),1,token_arg=(4,0,0,0))
contexts = [(3,0,0,0), (2,0,0,0)]
assert [covers(t,1) for t in contexts] == [True,False]
assert {('G','H'), ('H','G')} == {('H','G'), ('G','H')}
print('PASS: qinplace minimum, exact-token, args-domain and two-context counterexamples')

# Proposed wire-request example; arithmetic is independent of any runtime body.
mask = (1 << 257)-1
a, b = (1 << 256)+3, 2
assert ((a*b) & mask) == 6
assert a.bit_count() == 3 and (((a*b) & mask).bit_count()) == 2
assert [min(64,257-64*k) for k in range(5)] == [64,64,64,64,1]
entries = [{'op':'mul','width':257}, {'op':'ctpop','width':257}, {'op':'mul','width':257}]
canonical_entries = [{'op':op,'width':w} for op,w in sorted({(e['op'],e['width']) for e in entries})]
assert canonical_entries == [{'op':'ctpop','width':257},{'op':'mul','width':257}]
request = {'schema':'bennett-runtimes-instantiations-1', 'llvm_major':18,
           'target_triple':'x86_64-unknown-linux-musl', 'data_layout':'e-p:64:64',
           'entries':canonical_entries}
canonical = json.dumps(request,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')).encode()
print('Proposed request example SHA-256:',hashlib.sha256(canonical).hexdigest())
print('PASS: i257 top-limb arithmetic and canonical request example')

def run(argv, cwd=ROOT, timeout=20, expected=0):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=cwd, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=timeout)
    assert p.returncode == expected, (argv,p.returncode,p.stdout,p.stderr)
    return p
native = run([sys.executable,'-I','-B','-u',WS/'bennett-arith/tests/ppc_zero_low_witness.py'])
assert native.stdout.count('flags=0;') == 2
assert native.stdout.count('flags=24;') == 2
print(native.stdout, end='')
tests = run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','unittest','-v',
             'tests.test_replay.K13Witness','tests.test_replay.QinplaceCoverage'],cwd=REPLAY)
assert 'Ran 12 tests' in tests.stderr and tests.stderr.rstrip().endswith('OK')
print('PASS: independent replay focused tests, 12/12')
runtime = WS/'bennett-runtimes'
ll = runtime/'.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/tmp/runtime-width-request.ll'
for major in (18,23):
    tools = Path(f'/usr/lib/llvm-{major}/bin')
    run([tools/'opt','-passes=verify','-disable-output',ll])
    bc = runtime/f'dist/llvm{major}/bennett-runtimes.bc'
    nm = run([tools/'llvm-nm','--defined-only',bc]).stdout
    defs = {line.split()[-1] for line in nm.splitlines() if line.split()}
    assert not {'__bennett_rt_i257_mul','__bennett_rt_i257_ctpop'} & defs
    print('PASS: LLVM',major,'i257 source verifies; both definitions absent;',
          'runtime SHA-256',hashlib.sha256(bc.read_bytes()).hexdigest())

replay = json.loads(run([sys.executable,'-B','-m','checker_replay',
                        WITNESS/'certificate.json','--object',WITNESS/'object.bisa',
                        '--max-elements','16'],cwd=REPLAY).stdout)
claim = next(iter(replay['regions'].values()))['claims'][0]
assert (claim['outcome'],claim['elements'],claim['covered_fraction']) == ('proof',16,'1')
original = run([REF,'check',WITNESS/'object.bisa'],expected=1)
assert 'c must name one wire' in original.stderr
scratch = JOB/'tmp/mcx-ccx.bisa'
source = (WITNESS/'object.bisa').read_text()
old = 'e38d5da64f9a0ae89a66243bcbe5a321cb807a5bbabf83952b63d3465dfab346'
new = 'f53238499e07824b6c62385c33426d4bc56bfaf900df61264ec65e0589061f39'
assert source.count('mcx (c) p') == 1 and source.count(old) == 1
scratch.write_text(source.replace('mcx (c) p','ccx c[0] c[1] p').replace(old,new))
for command in ('check','hash'):
    result = json.loads(run([REF,command,scratch]).stdout)
    assert result['object_hash'] == 'c785ff3f310f968e2a26f68834e99ab892cf0d1bffe816cf26b17d45c33a65af'
    assert result['symbols']['a']['interface_hash'] == new
    assert result['symbols']['a']['closure_hash'] == new
for p,q,c in product(range(2),range(2),range(4)):
    result = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',f'{p},{q}','--in',str(c)]).stdout)
    assert result['mut'] == [q ^ int(c==3),p] and result['in'] == [c]
    back = json.loads(run([REF,'run-action',scratch,'a','--mut',
                          ','.join(map(str,result['mut'])),'--in',str(c),'--adjoint']).stdout)
    assert back['mut'] == [p,q] and back['in'] == [c]
print('PASS: mcx replay proof; original reference rejection; ccx check/hash and 16+16 executions')
print('PASS: derive3 verification complete; proposals remain unaccepted/unimplemented')
```

## Remaining decisions and ownership

Council/editor owners must ratify and publish these changes; the existing
five issues remain their tracking records. Arith still owes exact incoming
conversion/FMA mechanics and flags after the parity decision. Runtimes
and driver/plugin still owe the helper protocol and bodies. Checker and
executor owners must add the proposed context/operand vectors, and the
K13 editor must publish a complete corrected fixture. None of that
downstream implementation is claimed complete by this notes-only job.
# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). Binding interpretations of 0.2.12 itself (text and SHA
unchanged) are in [`errata-0.2.12.md`](errata-0.2.12.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# spec/

`Bennett-ISA-Spec.md` is the normative text: draft 0.8, **accepted by the
three-model council on 2026-10-28 (round 8, all members accept, no blocking
issues, mean 4.17/5)**. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett`
meta repo (`docs/council/isa-2026-10-28/`, `tools/council.py`).

Rules for changing it:

- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (section 1); drafts
  are not envelope-compatible, so the four example hashes in section 16.3
  change with it. Run `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py --repatch`
  then `python3 reference-interpreter/isa_check.py`.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every
  time it was tried.
- Add every new example or stated result to `isa_check.py` before anyone
  grades it.
- Section 17 lists decisions deferred to implementation with their
  defaults; an implementer who must choose something not covered there
  files an issue and the default goes into section 17 first.
- Revisions go back through the council.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4796:| N9 | `atomic` and `volatile` on loads and stores; `fence`; `atomicrmw`; `cmpxchg` | plain load/store; fence deleted; `atomicrmw op ptr p, T v` → `%nz.l.r = load T, ptr p`, `%nz.v.r = <op> T %nz.l.r, v` for `add`/`sub`/`and`/`or`/`xor` (for `xchg`: v; `min`/`max`/`umin`/`umax`: `icmp` + `select` named `%nz.k.r`, `%nz.v.r`; FP `fadd`/`fsub`: ordinary `fadd`/`fsub` then N32; `fmax`/`fmin`, `fmaximum`/`fminimum`, `fmaximumnum`/`fminimumnum`: respectively `llvm.maxnum`/`llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maximum`/`llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximumnum`/`llvm.minimumnum` then N32; `nand`: `%nz.a.r = and T %nz.l.r, v`, then `%nz.v.r = xor T %nz.a.r, -1` (these two logical instructions, in this order, without flags); `uinc_wrap`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, i=`add old,1`, new=`select k,0,i`; `udec_wrap`: k=`icmp eq old,0`, g=`icmp ugt old,v`, w=`or k,g`, d=`sub old,1`, new=`select w,v,d`; `usub_cond`/`usub_sat`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, s=`sub old,v`, new=`select k,s,old` / `select k,s,0`. Names are `%nz.{k,i,v}.r`, `%nz.{k,g,w,d,v}.r` and `%nz.{k,s,v}.r` respectively, all arithmetic at T without overflow flags), `store T %nz.v.r, ptr p`, uses of `%r` → `%nz.l.r`; `cmpxchg ptr p, T c, T n` → `%nz.l.r = load`, `%nz.e.r = icmp eq T %nz.l.r, c`, `%nz.s.r = select i1 %nz.e.r, T n, T %nz.l.r`, `store T %nz.s.r, ptr p`, `%nz.a.r = insertvalue { T, i1 } zeroinitializer, T %nz.l.r, 0`, `%r = insertvalue { T, i1 } %nz.a.r, i1 %nz.e.r, 1` (single-thread assumption, recorded in the certificate, §24 D24). spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6818:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6824:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6863:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6865:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6867:The arith distribution MUST provide a documented offline generator accepting that request and producing matching-major ordinary bitcode plus a JCS manifest containing `schema: "bennett-arith-instances-1"`, the request SHA-256 as `request_sha256`, `bitcode_sha256`, and `exports` (array of objects with exact `symbol` and LLVM `function_type` strings sorted by symbol); every requested definition and its reachable closure MUST be present, with no hardware FP or host libm import, and linked before normalisation identically in both builds. Default prebuilt exponent widths are a cache, never a capability bound; generation failure is an explicit implementation blocker, not a successful link, unsupported-format verdict, or silent narrower substitute.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6869:**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6871:N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6875:N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k). For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6914:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6918:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6920:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6922:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6928:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6933:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6942:C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table. No conversion through D is emitted.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6952:named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6974:runtimes/arith versions. No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6982:For `_rm` carrier transcendentals, implementations MUST choose a result within the same envelope on the requested side of the exact result for directed modes, use nearest-side selection for the two nearest modes, and raise IEEE invalid for a domain error or signaling NaN, divbyzero for a pole, overflow when the correctly rounded mathematical result overflows B, underflow when that result is tiny after rounding and inexact, and inexact iff the returned finite value differs from the exact mathematical result; approximation work alone MUST NOT set a flag on an exactly representable mathematical result. For nearest-side selection, compare the distances to the two B neighbours bracketing the exact result, with ties to even or away as requested; this constrained case is correctly rounded. Unconstrained carrier operations retain the one-ulp allowance. Flags for modf/powi follow their exact split/sequenced arithmetic semantics. The native-format musl libm accuracy policy remains the recorded, versioned musl implementation; the quantitative carrier envelope applies to every own-format carrier route.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6984:### 13.5 Constrained intrinsics, `strictfp` and the floating-point environment
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:7000:**Quiet and signaling comparison ABI.** N32 MUST lower `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmp` to `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)` and `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmps` to the distinct `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmps_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)`, with P exactly the 0..15 predicate encoding of §13.1 and R = 0 (these intrinsics have exception metadata but no rounding metadata); vector forms scalarise first, and no predicate-bit extension encodes signalingness.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4758:### 12.2 The rules
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4775:| N9 | `atomic` and `volatile` on loads and stores; `fence`; `atomicrmw`; `cmpxchg` | plain load/store; fence deleted; `atomicrmw op ptr p, T v` → `%nz.l.r = load T, ptr p`, `%nz.v.r = <op> T %nz.l.r, v` for `add`/`sub`/`and`/`or`/`xor` (for `xchg`: v; `min`/`max`/`umin`/`umax`: `icmp` + `select` named `%nz.k.r`, `%nz.v.r`; FP `fadd`/`fsub`: ordinary `fadd`/`fsub` then N32; `fmax`/`fmin`, `fmaximum`/`fminimum`, `fmaximumnum`/`fminimumnum`: respectively `llvm.maxnum`/`llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maximum`/`llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximumnum`/`llvm.minimumnum` then N32; `nand`: `%nz.a.r = and T %nz.l.r, v`, then `%nz.v.r = xor T %nz.a.r, -1` (these two logical instructions, in this order, without flags); `uinc_wrap`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, i=`add old,1`, new=`select k,0,i`; `udec_wrap`: k=`icmp eq old,0`, g=`icmp ugt old,v`, w=`or k,g`, d=`sub old,1`, new=`select w,v,d`; `usub_cond`/`usub_sat`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, s=`sub old,v`, new=`select k,s,old` / `select k,s,0`. Names are `%nz.{k,i,v}.r`, `%nz.{k,g,w,d,v}.r` and `%nz.{k,s,v}.r` respectively, all arithmetic at T without overflow flags), `store T %nz.v.r, ptr p`, uses of `%r` → `%nz.l.r`; `cmpxchg ptr p, T c, T n` → `%nz.l.r = load`, `%nz.e.r = icmp eq T %nz.l.r, c`, `%nz.s.r = select i1 %nz.e.r, T n, T %nz.l.r`, `store T %nz.s.r, ptr p`, `%nz.a.r = insertvalue { T, i1 } zeroinitializer, T %nz.l.r, 0`, `%r = insertvalue { T, i1 } %nz.a.r, i1 %nz.e.r, 1` (single-thread assumption, recorded in the certificate, §24 D24). Volatile accesses in platform-profile code are first routed by N35 (§20.3) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4790:| N17 | (0.1: FP formats and transcendentals → F(8)) | replaced by §13 (N32, N33): no floating-point construct faults |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4792:| N19 | floating-point arithmetic, comparison and conversion of `float` and `double` | the soft-float rewrite of §13.1 (applied by N32) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4795:| N22 | **wide integers and non-byte memory**: an integer type iN with N > 128 in an instruction; a load or store of iN with N not a multiple of 8 or of an aggregate type | iN with N > 128 becomes L = ⌈N/64⌉ i64 limbs (limb 0 the least significant; the top limb is i(N − 64(L−1))) with the expansions of §12.7; a load or store of iN with N mod 8 ≠ 0 becomes a load or store of i(8⌈N/8⌉) with a `trunc` after the load and a `zext` before the store (fixing LangRef's unspecified extra bits as zero); a load or store of an aggregate becomes one access per element in element order at its offset (`%nz.e<k>.<r>`) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4800:| N27 | **integer intrinsics** | the fixed expansions of §12.8 |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4805:| N32 | **floating point** | the soft-float rewrite of §13.1–§13.2 (after N21) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4842:**`n-defined-zero`.** N12 totalises the flags before N27. This does not exempt abs, ctlz or cttz from expansion: full materialised output contains no calls to these LLVM intrinsics, including false-flag calls. At x=0 both counts are 8, so the sum is 16; at x=1 they are 7 and 0, so the sum is 7.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6797:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6803:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6842:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6846:The arith distribution MUST provide a documented offline generator accepting that request and producing matching-major ordinary bitcode plus a JCS manifest containing `schema: "bennett-arith-instances-1"`, the request SHA-256 as `request_sha256`, `bitcode_sha256`, and `exports` (array of objects with exact `symbol` and LLVM `function_type` strings sorted by symbol); every requested definition and its reachable closure MUST be present, with no hardware FP or host libm import, and linked before normalisation identically in both builds. Default prebuilt exponent widths are a cache, never a capability bound; generation failure is an explicit implementation blocker, not a successful link, unsupported-format verdict, or silent narrower substitute.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6848:**Wide integer sides (M>128).** The preceding i128 conversion rows apply only
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6850:N32 emits `void @__bennett_sf_<op>_B_wide(ptr d, C operand, i32 M)`.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6854:N22 loads each limb at width min(64,M−64k). For sitofp/uitofp from iM, store
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6893:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6897:Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6901:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6907:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6912:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6921:C_B and suffix are exactly §13.1's table. No conversion through D is emitted.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6931:named operation numerically in B, using the same deterministic, versioned
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6953:runtimes/arith versions. No correct-rounding claim is required; bit-exact
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6961:For `_rm` carrier transcendentals, implementations MUST choose a result within the same envelope on the requested side of the exact result for directed modes, use nearest-side selection for the two nearest modes, and raise IEEE invalid for a domain error or signaling NaN, divbyzero for a pole, overflow when the correctly rounded mathematical result overflows B, underflow when that result is tiny after rounding and inexact, and inexact iff the returned finite value differs from the exact mathematical result; approximation work alone MUST NOT set a flag on an exactly representable mathematical result. For nearest-side selection, compare the distances to the two B neighbours bracketing the exact result, with ties to even or away as requested; this constrained case is correctly rounded. Unconstrained carrier operations retain the one-ulp allowance. Flags for modf/powi follow their exact split/sequenced arithmetic semantics. The native-format musl libm accuracy policy remains the recorded, versioned musl implementation; the quantitative carrier envelope applies to every own-format carrier route.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6963:### 13.5 Constrained intrinsics, `strictfp` and the floating-point environment
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6979:**Quiet and signaling comparison ABI.** N32 MUST lower `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmp` to `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmp_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)` and `llvm.experimental.constrained.fcmps` to the distinct `i1 @__bennett_sf_cmps_B_rm(C_B, C_B, i32 P, i32 R)`, with P exactly the 0..15 predicate encoding of §13.1 and R = 0 (these intrinsics have exception metadata but no rounding metadata); vector forms scalarise first, and no predicate-bit extension encodes signalingness.

(ii) Binding a `qreg[*]` to a `wire[n]` or `region[A,W]` slot checks the width at the call: CC trap, R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, on a mismatch. # Binding interpretations of accepted dialect 0.2.10

Published with draft 0.2.12, 2026-11-01. Accepted text at `023f5a771a814270634db3be9cfde88ded69f975`, spec SHA-256 `d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206`, is unchanged; consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations resolve ambiguity in that text; new 0.2.12 ABIs/accuracy requirements are identified separately and are not retroactive ABI changes.

## mkb — §§5.2, 7.2, 12.2 N0

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

## jjr — §§16.9, 17.1

The thin malloc body in §16.9 is a specialised witness whose calls all request 16 bytes. ISA allocation itself is exactly byte-bumping, not aligned; C malloc still owes its target ABI alignment. The general wrapper rounds its requested extent and inserts explicit charged padding allocations when an intervening runtime primitive leaves the next address unaligned, identically in both builds. This resolves the example-versus-C-ABI ambiguity without altering ISA §7.1.

## lbq — §§11.2–11.3, 14.7, 19.2

**Console syscall framing.** For virtual stdout/stderr, runtimes MUST implement `writev` by validating the vector count and total length before the first effect, then emitting one `console.write` per nonempty vector in source order (zero-length writes emit no record), returning the total byte count; the descriptor ABI limits are 0..1024 vectors and total at most target SSIZE_MAX, with invalid counts/overflow returning EINVAL and a null nonempty vector table returning EFAULT.

The console shim MUST return ENOTTY for `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, ESPIPE for seeking, and success for closing a virtual stdout/stderr descriptor, with no extra device effect or terminal metadata; this close releases no host resource and does not disable the virtual byte sink.

**Model history.** R console writes MUST use only the ISA effect tape, while L model state is ordinary reversible memory initialised from descriptor bindings, private and discarding only under the oracle rules of §11.3; action console capacity exhaustion MUST fault before mutation under TDL-12, and a standalone native-model test undo log MUST NOT substitute for either tier's history. No second production model-history ABI is required.

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

## 0na — §§3.1, 17.7, 19.5

The “nobody: primitives” ownership row applies to production bitcode before ISA lowering, not native comparison support implementing those primitives after materialisation. Such support must preserve device layout and history. Draft 0.2.12 §19.5 publishes the new v1 layout transport ABI; that spelling is not imposed retroactively on 0.2.10. The existing memory/fault outcomes and North Star definedness classifiers are not waived by UBSan-only support.

## dfd — §§13.1, 13.5

The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.

## 1av — §§13.4–13.5

“No correct-rounding claim” does not authorise an arbitrary deterministic non-numerical substitute for the named operation or waive the specified IEEE flags. The accepted text does not quantify its numerical error. The one-local-ulp carrier bound and precise constrained accuracy/flag policy in draft 0.2.12 are NEW requirements, not retroactive assertions that 0.2.10 contained a numeric bound or that measured sweeps prove a full-domain bound.

## z7u — §13.1 arbitrary-format row

“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

## 21a — §§13.1, 13.5

The generic “soft-float call of §13.1 with suffix _rm” does not merge the distinct LLVM quiet fcmp and signaling fcmps operations. Quiet-NaN invalid behavior must remain distinguishable. Draft 0.2.12 publishes the NEW cmps_B_rm spelling and exact operand/exception lowering in §13.5; the absence of an earlier spelling was a contract gap, not authority to silently substitute quiet comparison.
# Binding interpretations of dialect 0.2.12

Published 2026-11-02. Text of draft 0.2.12 at `c775e6d458006fde143f2bf8ec62f07b034fb431`, spec SHA-256 `cfcce70e84ead8a05924c0921f13fc9cb477fdd71f5448c7f6f357ea91349e83`, is unchanged; the full version string, map 0.2.9 and consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations clarify that text without changing any reference result. Draft 0.3 carries the same text in §§5.1, 5.2 and 24.

## 5u3 — §§5.1, 5.2, 24 (council run `contract-5u3-2026-11-02`, round 1 sol and codex accept; `accept-4ht-recheck-2026-11-02` round 1 PASS; muse accept in `accept-muse-batch-2026-11-02`; bead `b-dialect-spec-5u3`)

**§5.1, after K.** The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

**§5.2, after the least-fixpoint paragraph.** In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. For edges u→v labelled d_uv,
choose root-to-vertex path displacements p_v and let
g=gcd(2^W,{p_u+d_uv−p_v}). A seed a at u contributes
{a−p_u+p_v+kg mod 2^W} at v. Union contributions from all seeds.
Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.

**§24.** Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

# Bennett LLVM Dialect — next draft — OUTLINE 0.3

**Date:** 2026-10-31. **Status:** proposed outline for maintainer rulings; no draft text and no ratification implied. Prepared as the design sub-agent. Accepted baseline: dialect **0.2.10**, ISA **0.8**. Candidate target: ISA **0.9 draft**, accepted by sol/codex seats, muse held, K64/K65 awaiting maintainer ratification. This outline follows the structure and ruling convention of `outline-0.2.md`, while preserving the accepted text's subsequent council repairs (notably Appendix A).

**Scope:** all 40 open children of `b-dialect-spec-8wf`, the three requested dependency beads bz6/cbe/0t9, nonblocking PT12 bead 0y2, and the additionally relevant open ISA-wording bead gwf: **45 item records, 22 decisions**. The epic itself remains the scope ledger. Other open corpus/tooling siblings are not implicitly completed by this design.

**Reading key.** DLT IDs name `../bennett-conformance/clauses/DLT-0.2.10.statements.md`; ISA IDs name `ISA-0.9-draft.statements.md` there. An unsuffixed section or paragraph is a clause dependency; `.S<n>` selects a statement. K/E numbers are ISA §0 change-row mnemonics, not dependency IDs. NS is `../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md` iteration7, especially §3, §6.1 and the entire §8 ladder. Audit row IDs retain their original 0.1 meanings. “0.1 capability” below means the north-star capability the 0.1 narrowing removed or omitted, not a claim that restrictive 0.1 already implemented it. Every item distinguishes that history from the actual 0.2.10 state.

## Part A. Draft structure and release boundaries

Proposed section plan: retain §§1–25 and Appendices B/C; add an opening change ledger and dependency declarations; update §§3.4,5,6,8.4,9.5,11.5,17.5–17.6,19.9,23,25 for ISA 0.9; preserve the accepted normalisation/map unless an item explicitly changes it. Appendix A becomes a provenance ledger of departures from ratified outlines, retaining all accepted council repairs. There is no proposed change to marker ABI signatures or descriptor version2 in this outline.

Proposed editorial/process sentences (for the eventual draft, subject to D01):

- The next draft MUST preserve every north-star capability and every existing non-narrowing construction, with deferral limited to mechanics and any real missing ISA operation named precisely as a blocker. [deps: DLT-front-P3, DLT-1.3, DLT-1.6, DLT-24, DLT-25.1]
- Every new or changed normative sentence MUST end with a dependency declaration naming the DLT or ISA statement or clause IDs on which it relies. [deps: DLT-1.5, DLT-23]
- The 0.2.11 compatible track MUST retain the ISA 0.8 target, existing marker signatures, canonical map and externally visible reference behaviour while clarifying inherited rules and supplying their missing closure evidence. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-3.2, DLT-16, DLT-23]
- The 0.3 track MUST declare ISA 0.9 and its complete sub-version tuple, regenerate affected objects, canonical hashes, plugin identity, module flag, schedules and certificates, and preserve the accepted 0.2.10 ISA 0.8 artefacts as historical versioned vectors. [deps: DLT-16.2-P2, DLT-19.9, DLT-23, ISA-front-P2, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2, ISA-12.3]

The dependency-tag process rule additionally comes directly from repo CLAUDE.md rule12; its dependency marker above names the existing notation/versioning context, not an assertion that 0.2.10 already had rule12. Same principle for the no-narrowing standing rule (repo rule10, NS thesis).

Three groups below describe landing mechanics, never reduced scope:

- **0.2.11:** compatible/editorial confirmations and analysis clarifications already required by 0.2.10; these are mostly open-bead closure/evidence work, not newly invented capabilities.
- **0.3:** actual ISA 0.9 adoption, dynamic-register/token/R lowering and removal of obsolete dependency verdicts.
- **ISA-blocked:** bz6/cbe remain external adoption/implementation blockers in the accepted baseline, destined for 0.3 once the publication and execution gates pass; 0.9 contains their missing sentences already. This is not a claim of an additional ISA design gap.

## Part C — 0.2.11 item records

<a id="i01"></a>
### I01 — Taint-driven lifting

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.1`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D08. **Audit:** 1.4-d, D17, B-31, B-50, O-05.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 ran the classical part of ctrl/adj/compute once without controlling its escaping stores; the promised capability is inferred coherent execution of ordinary code.

**0.2.10 delta:** Already restored by DLT-7.1-P7 and DLT-10-P2 through DLT-10-P45: Cl/M/Co state, classical twins, coherent slices, store events and clean control workspace; no surviving generation-only restriction.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.7-P1, ISA-5.2, ISA-5.3 and ISA-6 provide controlled oracle calls, total XOR outcomes and cleanup; no extra ISA sentence is needed.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> An action body containing classical code MUST retain the Cl/M/Co partition, classical twins, predicated outliving stores and clean-workspace obligations of TDL-1 through TDL-12. [deps: DLT-10, DLT-18.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2, ISA-6]

**Rejected alternatives:** Generation-only execution changes the controlled store result; retaining incidental garbage as undeclared outputs violates the accepted cleanup contract.

**Conformance consequence:** DT/DS: retain tdl-a through tdl-d, co-loop-identity, co-loop-adj-overwritten, co-observe-write-observe and deferred-channel cases; add no subset rejection. Rungs 4/7/8 retain explicit-oracle paths.

**Dependency order:** I07, I17, I22, I33; I31 is the nested reversible-call case.

**Maintainer choice:** D08 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.
